‘Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping’ Cast Guide: All the Tributes, Mentors and Younger Versions of Characters in Prequel

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Before Katniss Everdeen ever volunteered as tribute for the Hunger Games, another District 12 victor made his way out of the arena, but with much fewer odds in his favor.

“The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping” is set 24 years before the first “Hunger Games” film (and 40 years after “The Ballad of Songbird and Snakes), following the 50th Hunger Games that crowned Haymitch Abernathy (Joseph Zada) as victor. To “celebrate” the second Quarter Quell, a higher-stakes edition of the games held every 25 years, the Capitol required twice the amount of children to participate, sending 48 tributes to the arena instead of the traditional 24.

With original stars Jennifer Lawrence, Josh Hutcherson and Woody Harrelson returning, the “Sunrise on the Reaping” brings together a star-studded lineup to play younger versions of some of the franchise’s well-known characters, including President Snow (Ralph Fiennes), Effie Trinket (Elle Fanning), Plutarch Heavensbee (Jesse Plemons), Caesar Flickerman (Kieran Culkin), Wiress (Maya Hawke) and Beetee (Kelvin Harrison Jr.). The film also introduces new characters such as fellow tribute Maysilee Donner (Mckenna Grace); District 12’s escort, Drusilla Sickle (Glenn Close) and stylist, Magno Stift (Billy Porter); and Haymitch’s girlfriend, Lenore Dove (Whitney Peak).

Part of Suzanne Collins’ best-selling book series, “Sunrise on the Reaping” is the sixth film of the franchise and arrives in theaters Nov. 20. Francis Lawrence and Nina Jacobson, who have collaborated on every “Hunger Games” film, return as director and producer, respectively. The most recent film, the President Snow prequel “The Ballad of Songbirds and Snakes,” was released in 2024 and starred Tom Blyth and Rachel Zegler.

See the full cast of “The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping” below.

Joseph Zada stars as Haymitch Abernathy, a tribute from District 12 who is the future mentor to the main series’ protagonist, Katniss Everdeen. Despite being born on the same day as the Reaping, Haymitch’s birthday luck runs short when he is selected for the second Quarter Quell and soon finds himself on President Snow’s radar after publicly defying the capitol.

Throughout the film, Haymitch is thrust into several intense moments, whether they are grounded in emotion or consist of demanding action sequences. One of Zada’s favorite scenes is a particular battle near the end of the film, which he tells Variety makes him “look like a fucking action star.”

“In terms of acting, I just want to throw myself completely in the deep end with every project. And it helps so much having that attitude in the arena, because that’s just my character the whole time,” Zada says. “He’s really going for it, and I was like, ‘There’s no way for me to do this believably unless I’m throwing myself down that mountain or pushing myself to the edge of death and almost injuring myself every single day. There was no other way for me to do that and make it believable without just completely diving in the deep end.”

Haymitch Abernathy was played by Woody Harrelson in the original films.

Joining Haymitch in the second Quarter Quell is District 12 tribute Maysilee Donner, who’s played by Mckenna Grace and is the original owner of Katniss’ mockingjay pin. Initially at odds with her fellow District 12 tributes, Maysilee eventually joins their alliance with the Newcomers and grows particularly close to Haymitch, encouraging him to defy the Capitol.

As a long-time fan of the “Hunger Games” franchise, Grace tells Variety she wanted her portrayal of Maysilee to be accurate to Collins’ source material. One of the things she prioritized was keeping the character’s anger consistent throughout the film instead of having her arc of “learning to be kinder diminish her anger.

“While she finds happiness in being kind to the people around her and protecting them, she’s still a very angry character. And I think that it’s really important to show young women and anyone in general that the two can coexist,” she says. “You can be very angry at the world, or you can be very loud about the things that you believe in and in standing up for yourself and the people around you. But you can also be kind throughout that.

Grace also portrays Merilee Donner, Maysilee’s twin sister. Her previous credits include “I, Tonya,” “Regretting You,” “The Handmaid’s Tale” and “Ghostbusters: Afterlife.” She is next set to star in “Scooby Doo: Origins” as a young Daphne Blake.

Lenore Dove Baird is Haymitch’s girlfriend and a descendant of the Covey, which Lucy Gray Baird (Rachel Zelger) was a part of in “Songbirds and Snakes.” After her mother died during childbirth, Lenore Dove was raised by her uncles Tam Amber and Clerk Carmine Clade, whom audiences met in the previous prequel. Whitney Peak plays the character after starring in films like “Molly’s Game” and series such as “Chilling Adventures of Sabrina.”

Ralph Fiennes stars as Coriolanus Snow, the cruel and tyrannical president of Panem who previously mentored Lucy Gray Baird, a District 12 tribute who won the 10th Hunger Games. After Haymitch publicly defies the Capitol, Snow marks him as a target and stops at nothing to suppress his resistance. No stranger to playing the villain, Fiennes has also starred as the antagonist in “The Menu” and as Voldemort in the “Harry Potter” franchise. Snow was played by Donald Sutherland in the original films and by Tom Blyth in “The Hunger Games: The Ballad of Songbirds & Snakes.”

Elle Fanning plays Effie Trinket, a Capitol stylist who steps in to help dress the District 12 tributes for their interviews. Effie ultimately becomes the district’s escort in the original films, where she was played by Elizabeth Banks. Earlier this year, Fanning received an Emmy nomination for “Margo’s Got Money Troubles” after previously starring in “A Complete Unknown,” “Maleficent” and “Super 8.”

Plutarch Heavensbee is a cameraman assigned to cover District 12 during the Hunger Games. Although he serves as a trusted advisor to President Snow, Plutarch is secretly involved in the growing rebellion against the Capitol and helps Haymitch execute his plan to break the arena. He’s played by Jesse Plemons, who also starred in “Bugonia,” “The Power of the Dog,” “Kinds of Kindness” and “Game Night.” Plutarch ultimately becomes a gamemaker for the third Quarter Quell in “Catching Fire,” where he is played by Philip Seymour Hoffman for the remainder of the original films.

As the host of the Hunger Games, Caesar Flickerman is responsible for keeping Capitol citizens entertained and invested throughout the annual event. Typically known for his bold outfits and quick-witted humor, the role was originated by Stanley Tucci and is now played by Kieran Culkin, whose previous credits include “Succession” and “A Real Pain.” Caesar’s grandfather Lucky (Jason Schwartzman) was the announcer in “Songbirds and Snakes.”

Drusilla Sickle is a Capitol chaperone for the District 12 tributes. Openly disdainful of the poor district, particularly Maysilee, Drusilla hopes to earn a promotion to a higher-ranking group of tributes and takes extreme measures to ensure her presentations are nothing short of perfection. Glenn Close stars as the vain escort after playing a similarly eccentric character in “101 Dalmatians.” She is also known for her roles in “Fatal Attraction” and “Dangerous Liaisons.”

Billy Porter plays Magno Stift, a reptile-loving Capitol stylist unwillingly assigned to the District 12 tributes. Magno puts little effort into dressing the tributes on the rare occasion he decides to show up. Porter is largely known for his stage career, having starred in Broadway shows including “Kinky Boots,” “Cabaret” and “Shuffle Along.”

After winning the 34th Hunger Games, Beetee secretly conspired against the Capitol, which President Snow eventually discovered. Now, Snow is punishing the District 3 victor by reaping his son, Ampert, for the second Quarter Quell. Kelvin Harrison Jr. plays the character after starring in films including “Mufasa: The Lion King” and “Elvis.” Jeffrey Wright originated the role in the previous films.

Also a fan of the franchise, Harrison Jr. tells Variety that, while he would refer to the books and Wright’s performance, he primarily focused on the world and character that the screenwriters of “Sunrise on the Reaping” created for that specific moment in time.

“What they [the screenwriters] gave me the real estate for was really fleshing out a sense of knowingness that there is no space for grief for him right now. He is in a position where there isn’t self-preservation,” Harrison Jr. says. “There is overcoming in order to have a larger cause to take care of a larger party, and what’s at stake is his own flesh and blood. And I wanted to figure out how to navigate the feeling of grief as something you can’t really control, but you’re trying to outrun it. But you really can’t.”

Harrison Jr. and Wright previously played father and son in 2018’s “Monster” and will do so again in “Samo Lives,” the upcoming Jean-Michel Basquiat biopic.

Wiress is the exceptionally intelligent victor of the 49th Hunger Games who, alongside Mags, mentors Haymitch, Maysilee, Wyatt and Lou Lou for the second Quarter Quell. She is played by Maya Hawke, who is known for her roles in “Stranger Things,” “Do Revenge” and “Inside Out 2,” and was originated by Amanda Plummer in “The Hunger Games: Catching Fire.”

Little is known about Wiress beyond the fact that her arena was filled with mirrors and that she won without killing a single tribute. Hawke tells Variety she “relished in” every piece of information she could find about the character, hoping to portray a version of Wiress who was “beautiful, curious, kind of shy and uniquely capable.”

“I imagine that she probably lost some allies and some friends in her games, and that emotionally attaching to these young people now would be a living nightmare,” Hawke says about Wiress’ relationships with the District 12 tributes. “It’s like I’m trying to see them as pigs, and could one of these pigs work for my protest? I’m trying not to see them as human, and what I tried to build in was that she slowly starts to see them as human, and that’s how she gets so messed up.”

Mags, the winner of the 11th Hunger Games from District 4, mentors the District 12 tributes for the 50th games. She is played by Lili Taylor, who also starred in “The Conjuring,” “Mystic Pizza” and “American Crime,” and was originated by Lynn Cohen in “The Hunger Games: Catching Fire.”

Taylor tells Variety she avoided relying too heavily on Cohen’s performance, instead focusing on who Mags was at this particular point in her life rather than the character’s eventual fate. Working with director Francis Lawrence, the two created a version of Mags that served as a motherly figure to her tributes.

“In some ways, Mags just really cares about those young mentees and will do whatever she can to help them, even under the circumstances,” she says. “It’s so strange when you really think about it deeply, but it’s sort of like they [the tributes] have a job to do, and I really like them, and I want them to do well.”

Jennifer Lawrence reprises her role as Katniss Everdeen, the protagonist of the original “Hunger Games” films. Reaped for the 74th games, the District 12 tribute became the face of the rebellion with her mockingjay pin, which was passed down from Maysilee Donner. Since joining the franchise in 2012, Lawrence has starred in films including “Silver Linings Playbook,” “Die My Love” and “No Hard Feelings.”

Peeta Mellark is Katniss’ fellow District 12 victor of the 74th Hunger Games and her love interest in the original films, making him a key role in the rebellion. Josh Hutcherson returns to the role 11 years after the release of “The Hunger Games: Mockingjay – Part 2″ and has since starred in the “Five Nights at Freddy’s” films and “I Love LA.”

The role of Haymitch Abernathy was originated by Woody Harrelson in the original films, where he served as the heavy-drinking, hard-shelled mentor to Katniss and Peeta. Secretly conspiring with Capitol insiders, Haymitch helped launch the start of the rebellion and oversaw the dethroning of President Snow. Harrelson’s additional credits include “Cheers,” “Brothers” and “Now You See Me.”

Proserpina Trinket is Effie’s younger sister and a Capitol prep team member assigned to Haymitch for the Quarter Quell. Played by Iris Apatow, the university student is constantly worrying her grade will be affected due to Magno’s failure to dress the District 12 tributes. Apatow’s previous credits include “Funny People,” “Knocked Up” and “This is 40.”

Working alongside Proserpina, Vitus is also a part of Haymitch’s Capitol prep team who is similarly apprehensive about how his grade will be affected. He is played by Edvin Ryding, who previously starred in “28 Years Later” and “Mother Goose.”

Ben Wang plays Wyatt Callow, the first male tribute from District 12 to be reaped. He and his family are widely known throughout the district as the “booker boys,” a name that reflects their illegal gambling business that takes bets on the annual Hunger Games. Wang plays the character after appearing in films like “The Long Walk,” “Karate Kid: Legends” and “Mean Girls.”

Molly McCann stars as Louella, Haymitch’s young neighbor in District 12 who is also selected to fight in the Quarter Quell. Often styled in two braids and having earned the nickname “sweetheart” by Haymitch, Louella offers many similarities to Katniss.

Iona Bell plays Lou Lou, a lookalike of Louella McCoy assigned by President Snow to serve as a District 12 tribute. Though her origins in the film are unknown, she was said to be from District 11 in Collins’ novel. This is Bell’s first major film role.

Ampert is a District 3 tribute who is also the son of former victor Beetee Latier. He created the Newcomers alliance that would outnumber the Careers and helped Haymitch with his plans inside the arena. Ampert is played by Percy Daggs IV, who has also acted in “Never Let Go” and “Paradise.”

Silka is one of the female Careers — a tribute who’s trained to compete in the games from a young age — from District 1. Played by Laura Marcus, Silka is set on targeting Maysilee from their early interactions during training. Marcus’ credits also include “Death by Lightning” and “The Jetty.”

Panache is one of two male tributes from District 1 and a member of the Careers who personally targeted Haymitch since their first interaction, physically attacking him during training and vowing to kill him in the arena. He’s played by Jhaleil Swaby, who also appeared in “Neagley” earlier this year.

Rada Rae plays Wellie, one of the two female tributes from District 6. Though her role in the film is relatively limited, Wellie forms a trusting friendship with Haymitch in the novel, with the protagonist vowing to protect her as much as he could in the arena. Rae previously starred in Netflix’s “Exterritorial.”

Ringina is a District 7 tribute who is a part of the Newcomers alliance. She is played by Tatyana Muzondo, who is making her film debut.

Salimou Thiam plays District 9 tribute Clayton, who joins the Newcomers alongside Haymitch and Ampert.

Smylie Bradwell stars as Haymitch’s younger brother, Sid, who is two years short of having his name entered in the annual reaping. Largely introduced to audiences via “Hamnet,” Bradwell has also starred in “Faith” and “The Crow Girl.”

Burdock is the future father of Katniss Everdeen and Haymitch’s best friend in District 12. Played by Scot Greenan, Burdock first began dating Asterid around the second Quarter Quell and is also Lenore Dove’s distant relative. Phillip Troy Linger played the character in the original films.

Asterid is the future mother of Katniss who first began dating Burdock during the events of the second Quarter Quell. She is also a close friend of the Donner twins, standing with them at the reaping and sharing an emotional goodbye after Maysilee’s name was called.

Opportunity Zone Funds Defer Capital Gains Tax – Tax-Free Growth Takes 10 Years

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Put a capital gain into an opportunity zone fund only if you’d reinvest that money anyway and can leave it there for 10 years. For money invested from 2027, the tax on the gain is put off for five years and trimmed, not erased. The fund’s own growth goes tax-free only after a decade.

The 2025 tax law that made opportunity zones permanent set those terms, and they start on Jan. 1, 2027, when the Treasury’s new zones take effect.

After you sell stock, a business, or property at a gain, you have 180 days to put that gain into a qualified opportunity fund, which invests in designated low-income areas called opportunity zones. The tax on the gain is deferred for five years. Then 10% of the gain goes untaxed, or 30% if the fund keeps at least 90% of its assets in rural zones. Hold the fund at least 10 years, and its growth is tax-free when you sell, though growth after its 30th year is taxed.

Say a fund at least holds its value, and you put a $200,000 gain from a 2027 stock sale into it. Your 2032 return reports $180,000 of that gain, and that tax comes due while the fund still holds your money. Sell after 10 years, in 2037 at the earliest, and the fund’s growth is tax-free.

The switch to the new rules makes timing matter. Gains already deferred under the old rules become taxable on Dec. 31, 2026, and IRS guidance bars rolling them into a new fund. A 2026 gain whose 180 days run into January 2027 can wait and go in under the new rules.

Only the gain qualifies, so any extra money you add counts as a separate investment with no tax break. Getting the full break also means leaving your money in projects in low-income areas for a decade, and the manager’s fees cut your return. The state securities regulators’ association warns that you could escape the tax and still lose your whole investment.

So judge the fund as an investment first. Read what it will build and where, look up its manager and anyone selling it to you on FINRA’s BrokerCheck, and get every fee in writing.

Before you sign, ask whether you’d buy the fund with no tax break, and walk away if the answer is no.

The 101 money moves that matter most, from the foundations to protecting what you’ve built.

Dudes Online Are So Mad That ‘Grand Theft Auto 6’ Makes Them Respect Women

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Grand Theft Auto games never actually taught someone how to knock over a bank or survive a shootout with police, but the latest storyline in the franchise serves up some valuable lessons about what it means to be in a loving and committed relationship.

This isn’t sitting well with a histrionic minority of right-wing gamers who have been warning for months that GTA VI would be intolerably “woke.” While their attempt at a manufactured backlash is going even more poorly than the one that greeted Christopher Nolan’s film The Odyssey this summer (the day of its official release, the game is already closing in on 20 million copies sold and $2 billion in revenue), these streamers and commentators are throwing tantrums about gender politics anyway.

“RIP GTA,” one X user somberly concluded. “Killed by women.”

In case you’re among the handful of skeptics who didn’t launch their single-player campaign in GTA VI at midnight last night after 13 years of desperate anticipation: This latest installment in the satirical crime-caper series is led by dual protagonists, Jason and Lucia, a couple whose meet-cute involves them both trying to jack the same car. Living together in a fictionalized version of South Florida, they look to get by on the wrong side of the law—but the player must also negotiate their emotional bond through domestic chores and acts of affection.

“This game is such woke slop,” wrote one blue-check user on X who has posted almost nothing besides negative reactions to the game for the past week. The post included a screenshot of a fully optional GTA VI mission that describes how the Lucia character will appreciate a flower delivery and indicates with a heart icon that the action will strengthen feelings between her and Jason.

Other choices you can make as Jason to get closer to Lucia (sex is very much on the table) include picking up groceries, getting a haircut or beard trim, gifting her a piece of jewelry, and repainting walls in the house.

The concept of role-playing as a nice and non-performatively masculine boyfriend has come as a rude shock to those more interested in explosions and mayhem. The right-wing streamer Zack Hoyt, known online as Asmongold, was dumbfounded to find himself unclogging a sink in a scene where Jason is trying to collect back rent from a tenant who refuses the money until the plumbing is fixed. “I actually have to fucking fix this bitch’s sink?” Hoyt asked on stream. “Why can’t she do this?” Elsewhere, when running errands for Lucia as Jason, Hoyt referred to GTA VI as a “cuck simulator.” (That Hoyt thinks caring for a romantic partner makes someone a cuckold is perhaps an unfortunate glimpse into his dating history.)

More frustrated responses to the massively popular GTA VI came from looksmaxxing influencer Braden Peters, aka Clavicular, (the game required too much “simping for girls,” the streamer, currently facing multiple rape charges, complained on X), anti-woke gamer activist Mark Kern, aka Grummz (he called the housework “mundane crap”), and misinformation superspreader Dom Lucre, who falsely claimed that Rockstar had specifically made it impossible to open fire in a gay nightclub. (Washington Post critic Gene Park pointed out that the club in question was not LGBTQ-specific and that the mechanics of the game don’t allow you to shoot anyone in any kind of club.) These comments came alongside the predictable gripes over supposedly “overweight” female characters with insufficiently large breasts.

If the Tates, incels, and other far-right reactionaries of the world are so bent out of shape, it’s probably because whereas GTA once punched down at marginalized groups like sex workers and transgender people, bigots and self-styled alpha males are now the butt of the joke. The Vice City of GTA VI is bristling with parodies of toxic manosphere content and crypto scams. Random characters on the street will spout MAGA-coded conspiracy theories and call you a “libtard” or “commie socialist” if you walk into them. These touches seem to have made the game into “a bug zapper for chuds,” as one Bluesky user put it. (That hasn’t stopped Republicans and Trump allies from trying and failing to score political points with the game.)

Yet the provocation of asking guys to consider acts of service for a virtual girlfriend could prove the most revolutionary element of GTA VI.

It’s not that games like The Sims haven’t explored this territory before, and Rockstar’s own Red Dead Redemption franchise paid similar attention to quotidian detail. In those frontier-set narratives, your cowboy character had to make coffee over campfires and even clean up manure in barns. By addressing characters’ most basic needs—you can even manage personal hygiene by showering—GTA VI recalls some of the slower and quieter aspects of that franchise.

What’s truly remarkable, though, is that adult men who grew up indulging their most selfish and sociopathic instincts in the older GTA games have had their nostalgia for those escapades cleverly complicated with real-life problems. They can still steal cars, sell drugs, and rise through the ranks of a seedy underworld. That will always be the appeal of the series, which is now asking eternally aggrieved culture warriors to consider a vital question: What if you didn’t have to do it alone?

California attorney general issues Nvidia subpoena in probe of China chip diversion

📅 2026-11-19  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

California Attorney General Rob Bonta issued an investigative subpoena to Nvidia on Thursday as part of a probe of whether the company's advanced semiconductor chips ended up in China in contravention of U.S. export restrictions, his office said.

The probe comes a week after former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang resigned after allegations he had multiple affairs, including one with Alice Huang, a suspected Chinese spy. Alice Huang, who is not related to Jensen Huang, was an executive at Megaspeed, a Singapore-based company that is being investigation for potentially diverting restricted Nvidia chips to China.

"The conduct of Nvidia and its former CEO raises bright red flags, including about the ways in which the company may have misled investors," Bonta said in a statement.

"My office is investigating whether Nvidia or Jensen Huang's actions resulted in China obtaining advanced chips or confidential information — and if Nvidia misled investors and the public about this risk, in violation of the law," Bonta said.

"Nvidia is in a messy, transnational situation but the underlying principle of our corporate securities law is clear: Companies cannot mislead their investors," the attorney general said.

Nvidia did not immediately respond to a request for comment on Bonta's subpoena.

Nvidia shares are down 22% in the nine trading days since Huang admitted to an extramarital affair. The stock is on pace for its seventh straight weekly loss, shedding 38% of its value over that stretch.

Gap names new Athleta CEO, lowers full-year guidance

📅 2026-11-19  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Gap Inc. on Thursday announced Ashley Kechter will be the next president and CEO of its Athleta brand, effective Dec. 14.

Kechter comes to Gap from activewear retailer Vuori, where she was global president and oversaw the brand's global business strategy, marketing and financials. She was previously global brand president of Fabletics and began her career at Gap before that.

"I'm excited to return to Gap Inc. and partner with [Gap CEO Richard Dickson] and the team to strengthen our connection with customers and shape the brand's next chapter," Kechter said in a statement.

The announcement comes after Lululemon poached former Athleta CEO Maggie Gauger to be its chief product officer last month.

"Clearly, we're disappointed with Athleta's performance, and we recognize there's a lot of work ahead," Dickson told CNBC. "We had been in rebuild mode, but now we're really in turnaround mode."

Dickson said the company is focused on getting back to its fundamentals: fabrications, feature, form, function and fit. Dickson added that Kechter will be conducting a "comprehensive review" of the brand to reposition Athleta for future success.

While the execution of its strategy has thus far "fallen short," Dickson said this new approach will allow Athleta to fully realize its potential.

Gap also reported mixed fiscal third-quarter earnings on Thursday and posted flat overall comparable sales. Wall Street had been expecting a comparable sales increase of 1.3%, according to StreetAccount.

Net sales grew 2%, though demand softened in October and early November due to warmer weather, the company said. Store sales increased 1%, while online sales increased 3%.

Gap also said it's recorded a $420 million adjustment to cost of goods sold and a $21 million adjustment to interest income from tariff refunds so far this year.

The company lowered its full-year net sales outlook to be "roughly flat," down from previous guidance of up between 1% and 1.5%. Gap is also now expecting adjusted earnings per share of between $2.29 and $2.34, down from an earlier expectation of between $2.35 and $2.45 per share.

Dickson said in an interview that much of the lowered guidance was due to warmer weather in the first two weeks of the quarter, but he said Gap is expecting "meaningful acceleration" in shopping ahead of the peak holiday season.

"We're monitoring the trends, and we're going to remain agile as we respond to the changing business trends," Dickson said.

Here's how the company performed in its fiscal third quarter compared with what Wall Street was expecting, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

For the quarter, Gap reported net income of $311 million, or 86 cents per share, compared with $236 million, or 62 cents per share, the year prior. Sales rose slightly from $3.94 billion the year prior.

At Athleta, net sales were down 14% compared with last year, coming in at $220 million. Comparable sales for the banner sank 15%, which the company attributed to "limited traction" from efforts to modernize its merchandise and marketing.

The company's namesake brand saw 9% comparable sales growth for the quarter, with net sales of $1.1 billion, marking an 11% increase compared with last year. Dickson said it was the brand's 12th consecutive quarter of positive comparable sales.

"Gap is really firing on all cylinders, and the consistency of the brand is really giving us confidence in the brand's ability to continue its momentum," Dickson said.

The Old Navy banner saw flat comparable sales for the quarter and net sales of $2.3 billion, up 1% compared with last year. It's the first earnings report since the brand, which has been experiencing sluggish sales, named Michael Francis as its new CEO last quarter. Francis began his role at Old Navy on Nov. 2.

Banana Republic saw its net sales sink 3% compared with last year, which the company attributed to delayed demand for cold-weather categories and a women's fall assortment that fell flat. Wall Street had been expecting growth of 1.5%, according to StreetAccount.

Dickson said the brand has since introduced new products and seen demand "rebound" as cooler weather has set in across the country.

"We've been encouraged by the early customer response, and we should see sequential improvement in the fourth quarter," he said. "There's some work to do here, but with peak selling still ahead, I'm confident that the team is focused on the right areas and [will] ultimately position Banana well for growth."

Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: How can one do Tax-loss harvesting on index funds? Large 100%+ capital gains, not-married, no property or loan

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Can we implement this personally. Does it have to be individual stocks. How can we do manually using just TLH pairs like VTI, VOO etc. Would options like puts and calls be used for long and short positions ?
I am a big believer in DIY. However....

It is complex. One needs a firm grasp of the tax code and portfolio theory. The portfolio theory relies heavily on upper level statistics and liner algebra. As a side bar you are going to need access to quality data.

It requires discipline. Frequent monitoring (at least weekly) and rebalancing/harvesting (at least every 6 weeks). As such, it requires time. And time is money.

In theory it is safe. Theory tends to unravel during times of crisis. So you need a game plan and even more discipline to act during times of crisis.

You want pairs with high Beta and low correlations. This argues for individual stocks. Options are in some way better. But that means you need to know option theory. So another level of complexity. And trading options is more expensive. Plus they tend to be more lumpy. So while the power is higher for a given dollar invested, it is also less precise for smaller portfolios.

Now, would I feel comfortable doing this? eh. I have done this professionally, so I am familiar with the tools and concepts. In my younger days yes. I do love a good spreadsheet. Today - well, I would rather spend time with my kid.

New Homes Offer More Affordability for Buyers: Report

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

With affordability remaining an issue at large in housing, new homes continue to represent a tempting alternative in a challenging market.

The latest data from the National Association of Home Builders (NAHB) found that affordability for new homes actually improved more than that of existing homes in Q3, focused on both home prices and rates.

The Q3 NAHB/Wells Fargo Cost of Housing Index (CHI) found that families earning the national median income of $106,800 needed 33% of their income to buy a median-priced new home, compared to 36% of their income for a median-priced existing home.

Low-income families (those who earn only 50% of the national median income) need 66% of their income to buy a new home, compared to 73% for an existing home.

NAHB Chairman Bill Owens noted that this was the “widest affordability advantage (for new homes) since the CHI began,” as builders “are working hard to offset rising costs.”

The report also noted that this is due in part to the difference in price between new homes ($400,400 median price) and existing homes ($416,700 median price), called out as the widest national median price gap since the CHI began.

The other attribution for the new home “affordability advantage” is the incentives and deals offered by builders.

Owens called out NAHB’s recent data on builder confidence, which saw 43% of builders offer price cuts in October—the highest share since 2020. Additionally, 65% of builders offered sales incentives.

A recent Realtor.com® report also found that many buyers purchasing new homes are seeing lower mortgage rates due to builders offering buy downs or other deals with mortgage lenders. The average starting mortgage rate of a new-home buyer was 5.01% in Q3, compared with 6.15% for existing-home buyers.

While new homes are offering better affordability than existing homes, overall housing affordability is still very much challenged.

For both new and existing homes, families earning the national median income remain fairly cost-burdened (paying more than 30% of their income for housing) or severely cost-burdened (paying more than 50% of their income for housing).

Of the 175 metro areas tracked in the report, 87 are cost-burdened by housing, and eight are severely cost-burdened. Leading the way among the eight severely cost-burdened markets is San Jose, California, where families use 79% of their income on housing.

Owens outlined that in order to move housing affordability in the right direction, “policymakers must do more to reduce red tape, address labor shortages and fix supply chains so builders can expand the nation’s housing supply.”

“As builders contend with rising construction costs, tighter lending standards and persistent labor shortages, policymakers should remove regulatory obstacles, enact permitting reform and create a stronger business environment that enables builders to produce more attainable, affordable homes,” added NAHB Chief Economist Robert Dietz.

Claudia Larsen is a senior editor for RISMedia.

Plan for Long-Term Care in Your 50s – Your Savings Decide How You’ll Pay

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Medicare doesn’t pay for long-term care, so in your 50s, find out what care costs where you live while your health still qualifies you for coverage. Then choose how you’d pay by what you’ve saved: your own money if you’re wealthy, Medicaid if your assets are modest, and long-term care insurance if you’re in between.

Almost 70% of people turning 65 will need long-term care, meaning help with bathing, dressing, and other daily tasks, according to the Administration for Community Living. One in five will need it for more than five years.

Your age and health set what insurance costs and whether you can buy it. Most individual long-term care policies require a health review, so a diagnosis in your 60s can raise your price or rule you out.

The American Association for Long-Term Care Insurance’s 2026 price index shows a couple, both 55, paying about $5,000 a year combined, on average. That buys each of them a policy whose care benefits start at $165,000 and grow 3% a year. At 65, the same coverage averages about $7,000 a year.

Start by looking up typical prices in your area with CareScout’s cost of care calculator. Nationally, CareScout’s 2025 survey puts an in-home caregiver 44 hours a week at $80,080 a year, assisted living at $74,400, and a private nursing home room at $129,575.

Talk with your family, too, about who would give care and whether you’d stay in your own home or move to a facility. Ask your parents the same questions about their own care.

Get quotes for both kinds from an independent agent who sells several insurers’ policies. Identical coverage for an Illinois couple, both 60, ranged from $4,591 to $7,173 a year across five insurers in the same price index.

Tubi’s upcoming horror-comedy “Do You Like Me?” has cast Josh Server (“All That,” “The Neighborhood”), Yasmine Sahid (“Never Have I Ever Spent the Winter with the Romano Brothers”), Tyler Gaca and Alexander Huntley.

Ben Proudfoot’s acclaimed documentary “The Eyes of Ghana” has reached 1 million views on IShowSpeed’s YouTube channel in a single day.

The Breakwater Studios, Higher Ground and IShowSpeed production was released on YouTube on Tuesday following a livestream announcement the previous day between filmmakers Ben Proudfoot and Nana Adwoa Frimpong, Ethan Lewis from Higher Ground, Yaw Amponsah Apenteng, subject Chris Hesse, President Barack Obama and IShowSpeed.

The documentary follows Ghanaian cameraman Chris Hesse’s mission to rescue a hidden film archive documenting the birth of African independence. It is produced by IShowSpeed under his name Darren Watkin, directed by Ben Proudfoot and executive produced by President Barack Obama and Michelle Obama.

“Ghana and Africa mean so much to me,” IShowSpeed said in a statement. “Chris Hesse’s story matters and we’re not waiting on anybody to tell us which stories matter. We’re bringing this film to the world, for free. Chris Hesse spent his life protecting this important history. He deserves this moment. Ghana deserves this moment.”

Indie Distribution Exec Kyle Greenberg Exiting Utopia To Launch New Consultancy, Greenhouse

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Kyle Greenberg, longtime head of marketing and distribution at Utopia, is departing the company to launch a consulting startup called Greenhouse.

His new venture plans to develop highly curated, hands-on release strategies for filmmakers, artists, labels and brands to support their own efforts in self-distribution, something an increasing number of producers are turning to given challenges in the current indie film landscape.

Utopia was co-founded by Robert Schwartzman and Cole Harper in 2018. Greenberg helped launch the indie distribution, production and sales company, and its service-based Circle Collective division and has been a key player in growing both, overseeing creative, publicity and marketing, theatrical, home video, physical media and streaming for over 100 films. Related Stories News Utopia's Circle Collective Takes North American To 'Honeyjoon' Acquisitions Utopia's Circle Collective Acquires Greg Vrotsos' Silver Lake-Set 'Situations'

He championed breakout and award-winning features from first-time and auteur filmmakers like Emma Seligman (Shiva Baby), Jane Schoenbrun (We’re All Going To The World’s Fair), Kristoffer Borgli (Sick Of Myself), Sean Price Williams (The Sweet East), Amalia Ulman (El Planeta), Alex Ross Perry (Pavements), Gaspar Noe (Vortex), Anton Corbijn (Squaring The Circle, The Story of Hipgnosis), Errol Morris (American Dharma), Ali Abbasi (Holy Spider), the Ross Brothers (Bloody Nose, Empty Pockets) and others. He worked with Francis Ford Coppola to market and book Megalopolis, and with Mike Figgis on Megadoc. Watch on Deadline

His rollouts often merged non-traditional marketing and distribution, including bringing films to non-theatrical spaces with expansive elements like DJ sets, musical performances, art shows, pool parties and movie-date meetups, with a traditional, platformed theatrical or event strategy to build a footprint for independent and arthouse feature films across the U.S., U.K. and Canada.

“While so many people in the industry are lamenting a lack of reliable distribution methods, Greenhouse will be an extension of the ethos that has served as the foundation of my career — growth through disruption,” he said.

Greenberg is based in LA. He’ll be with Utopia through year end.

He also plans to scale up his own production label Stupid Co, created in 2024, alongside Greenhouse. Founded as a showcase for original work produced by Greenberg and partner Anna Maguire, Stupid Co expanded to events and experiences Cannes Dogs, Off-Viff and info labs Stupid Talks. It will release Greenberg and Maguire’s co-directed feature Galicia! in 2027. The hybrid documentary-eco horror comedy, shot on location at a winery in Spain, premiered at The Downtown Festival in NYC last month. The couple’s short films Hi! You Are Currently Being Recorded (2024) and French Lessons (2025) played about 70 festivals including Fantasia and SXSW Sydney.

In HelloNation, Business Performance Expert Martin Rowan of Naperville, IL, Explains How SAP Execution Impacts Operational Stability

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

NAPERVILLE, Ill., Nov. 19, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- What happens when SAP stops being used merely to explain results and starts being used to drive them? That question is addressed in a HelloNation article featuring insights from Martin Rowan, Managing Partner at Reveal USA, Inc. in Naperville, IL. The article explores how organizations can strengthen operational stability by moving beyond using SAP as a simple reporting layer and fully leveraging it for both planning and execution.

The article notes that many businesses still treat SAP as a reporting layer, pulling data into spreadsheets to make decisions. This practice, while common, often leads to fragmented workflows and reduced accountability. As highlighted in the HelloNation piece, when planning and execution occur outside SAP, teams face longer decision-making delays and greater inconsistencies across departments. This increase in decision latency can negatively affect overall business performance, leaving organizations to react to problems rather than prevent them.

According to the article, SAP execution becomes far more effective when it is positioned at the center of both planning and operational processes. Rowan explains that teams relying on spreadsheets miss out on version control, automated checks, and system-wide integration. These gaps lead to avoidable errors, duplicate work, and misaligned strategies. The article underscores that restoring execution in SAP enhances control, speeds decision-making, and enables proactive management of business operations.

The HelloNation article emphasizes that a fully integrated SAP environment improves both decision speed and outcome reliability. When SAP execution is prioritized, teams can access real-time information that empowers them to act faster and with more confidence. This operational visibility also increases accountability, as everyone works from the same data source. Business performance becomes easier to manage because processes are consistent, and service levels stabilize across the board.

Another important takeaway from the article is the financial benefit of aligning planning and execution within SAP. Companies can free up working capital by streamlining procurement and inventory strategies. When execution decisions are based on current SAP data, rather than disconnected spreadsheet estimates, unnecessary expenditures are reduced. SAP becomes not only a planning tool but a driver of financial efficiency and operational effectiveness.

As the article explains, the shift from using SAP as a reporting layer to a full planning and execution system requires more than just technology. It demands a cultural shift within organizations. Teams must be willing to trust SAP to manage operational tasks in real time. With this shift, companies reduce decision latency, improve transparency, and make faster, more informed decisions. This kind of commitment leads to measurable gains in business performance and long-term stability.

The HelloNation feature also highlights that employees benefit from this transformation. By eliminating the need to reconcile conflicting spreadsheets, teams can focus on tasks that add value to the business. With SAP execution in place, operations become more predictable and efficient, improving the experience for both employees and customers alike.

The article titled "When SAP Stops Explaining Results and Starts Delivering Them" features insights from Martin Rowan, Business Performance Expert in Naperville, IL, in HelloNation.

NFL predictions: Our picks for Giants-Jaguars, the full Week 11 slate

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The New York Giants face major test this week when they face the Jacksonville Jaguars. It is also a game that will provide a huge swing in the Giants' expected chances to make the playoffs. Will the Giants upset Jacksonville? See what our Big Blue View staff members think about that game and the rest of the Week 11 action in this week's staff picks.

I think there's a path to a win for the Giants, if they can find it. The Giants are one of the very best teams in the NFL at forcing drives to end in punts. At the same time, the Jaguars will be without their starting center and the Giants are one of the best teams in the League at creating quick pressure. That's important because Trevor Lawrence has been one of the quarterbacks most affected by pressure in the NFL.

At the same time, the Giants have rediscovered the explosive pass with J.J. McCarthy, which has been the weakness of the Jags' defense.

If the Giants can score touchdowns while disrupting Lawrence, they've got the chance to come away with the upset. We've seen the Giants rebound after bad games, as well as play much better than anticipated when they're underdogs. Maybe both will happen here.

The flip side of the coin is simpler and scarier to me: The Giants have one of the worst red zone defenses in the NFL, and the Jaguars have one of the best red zone offenses in the league. If the Giants can't stop them between the 20s, this game could get ugly fast.

Jacksonville is one of the hottest teams in the league with a young and explosive offense that can run the football. Their defense is aggressive but doesn't NEED to bring the blitz — they blitz at about a league-average rate, but Anthony Campanile does an excellent job utilizing simulated pressure to harass opposing quarterbacks. Jacksonville is third in offensive EPA, 4th in defensive EPA allowed, and 1st in Net EPA — they're a juggernaut based on their performance and the advanced analytics.

Still, that doesn't mean the Giants don't have a chance — they just must play efficient football and avoid the catastrophic mistakes that have robbed them of wins. It's possible, but against the most efficient team in the NFL, it will be difficult. John Harbaugh will have the Giants ready and prepared for the game, and I believe it will be more exciting and competitive. Jacksonville is concluding their third straight road game, which could give the Giants a slight advantage at home, but I ultimately believe the better team finds a way to win.

It took 11 weeks, but for the first time this season, the Giants will face a team with a winning record when the 7-2 Jaguars come to town on Sunday. Not only is Jacksonville running away with the AFC South (they lead the division by four games), but they're steamrolling the competition. They've won their last three games by a combined 75 points. This is easily Big Blue's toughest test of the season, so far.

Jacksonville is very good on both sides of the ball. They're fourth in offensive DVOA, third in defensive DVOA, and third in overall DVOA. They also have an excellent field goal kicker with crazy range. If you look at both rosters, it's hard to find clear advantages for the Giants at any position group outside of tight end, and even there, Jacksonville has good players.

Can the Giants win this game? Yes. They're at home, they're getting an extra three days of rest, and as we saw when they played Houston, they aren't scared and can surprise as a big underdog. They'll need to dial it up on defense and pressure Trevor Lawrence into the kinds of mistakes he used to make before Liam Coen got there. They also might have a little extra motivation after letting last week's game against the Commanders slip away at the end.

I won't be surprised if John Harbaugh's team hangs in there and keeps it as a one-score game most of the way. But in the end, until the Giants show me that they can beat a really good team, I can't pick them to do so.

You can talk yourself into believing that the Giants can beat Jacksonville this Sunday. They needed a miraculous last-second 57-yard field goal to beat the Eagles team (with Jalen Hurts) that the Giants defeated (without Hurts). They lost to the Texans team that the Giants beat the following week. The woeful Titans were within a TD against them last week with 5 minutes left. So indeed, it could be a competitive game. Unfortunately, the Jags have also won games by 24, 29, 35, and 23 points this season. They have scored 31 or more in each of their past four games, and over 40 in two of those. The Giants have only scored more than 30 points once, and only then because of a 99-yard pick-6 on the last play of the game.

Trevor Lawrence isn't necessarily playing any better this year than in the past. He's a QB that simultaneously makes great passes and puts the ball up for grabs, yet unlike in years past, he's cut down on the INTs while getting the TDs. Part of that may be due to 5'10" wide receiver Parker Washington (a 2023 6th-round pick), who had a breakout 2025 and is on pace for 1,000 yards. Running back Bhayshul Tuten, a 4th round 2025 pick, isn't quite in the Jahmyr Gibbs – Kenneth Walker class but he's not far below and is a missed tackle machine. A lot of the credit has to go to head coach Liam Coen, who also calls the plays and turned this team around as soon as he was hired. Lawrence, like most QBs, has trouble with pressure, but he is not too fazed by the blitz. Finding a way to disrupt his rhythm is a key to any chance the Giants may have.

On defense, the Jaguars are strong at linebacker with fourth-round 2023 pick Ventrell Miller, one of the league's best (note how many mid-round picks the Jags have hit on), along with Foyesade Oluokun. They also put on a decent pass rush with Josh Hines-Allen, Travon Walker, and Dennis Gardeck. In the secondary, Travis Hunter is playing more like a No. 2 pick, having mostly given up his evening gig as a receiver.

Are the Giants a serious playoff contender? This is the type of game they can use to make a statement against a team that is in the Super Bowl conversation. I think they'll put up a fight. I also don't think they're there yet.

I want to convince myself to pick the Giants to defeat the Jaguars. I really do. I just can't. The Jaguars are a team with justifiable Super Bowl ambitions. They are a well-rounded team with a high-powered offense, a very good defense, and one of the best field-goal kickers in the business in Cam Little.

The Giants are a good team. They are resilient. They will give the Jaguars their best shot. Maybe, just maybe, it will be good enough to pull off an upset that would go a long way toward ensuring that the Giants make the playoffs.

2 Growth Stocks to Buy in November Without Any Hesitation

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Vertex and Regeneron started 2026 slowly, but blockbuster results and deep pipelines have both stocks climbing again.

By Prosper Junior Bakiny – Nov 19, 2026 at 12:43PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Vertex Pharmaceuticals ›

Vertex Pharmaceuticals (VRTX -1.91%) and Regeneron (REGN -0.81%) did not have a strong start to the year -- both biotech giants were in decline for much of the first half of 2026. However, they have rebounded, and as of this writing, both have delivered above-average returns this year. They haven't peaked yet: Vertex Pharmaceuticals and Regeneron arguably remain excellent stocks to buy this month and hold onto over the medium term. Let me explain.

Vertex Pharmaceuticals could soon receive approval for a brand-new product, povetacicept. This investigational medicine for IgA nephropathy (a kidney disease) performed well in a Phase 3 study earlier this year, and regulators in the U.S. are expected to decide whether to approve it or not by the end of November. Povetacicept would be a nice addition to Vertex's lineup, which is still dominated by its cystic fibrosis (CF) medicines.

The drugmaker derives most of its revenue from CF therapies, an area where it is the undisputed leader. In the third quarter, Vertex's total revenue increased by 12% year over year to $3.44 billion. Vertex is unlikely to face stiff competition, generic or otherwise, within its core CF market anytime soon. But newer medicines, like povetacicept, will help expand and diversify its lineup.

Povetacicept could be the first of several drugs across kidney diseases for which Vertex earns approval. The company's pipeline also includes inaxaplin, an investigational medicine for APOL1-mediated kidney disease that is currently undergoing late-stage studies. Then there is VX-407, which is being developed to treat autosomal dominant polycystic kidney disease; VX-407 is in Phase 2 studies. Between them, these three drugs could eventually generate over $10 billion in peak annual sales, according to some estimates, which isn't that far off from the annual revenue it currently makes from its CF franchise.

Further, Vertex recently expanded its lineup and pipeline through the $10 billion acquisition of Crinetics Pharmaceuticals. The biotech estimated that the products it acquired through this buyout could eventually generate more than $5 billion in peak annual sales. And we haven't considered the company's newer launches, such as Casgevy, a gene-editing medicine for two rare blood diseases, and Journavx, a therapy for acute pain. Vertex boasts several other pipeline candidates, too. Vertex's prospects look very attractive as it continues posting excellent results while making meaningful clinical and regulatory progress.

Regeneron's financial results have smashed expectations this year, powered by a strong performance from its biggest growth driver, Dupixent, a medicine for eczema that it co-markets with Sanofi (SNY -2.36%). In the third quarter, Regeneron's revenue increased by 36% year over year to $5.1 billion. That was the fastest quarterly year-over-year growth rate the biotech company has recorded in years.

REGN Revenue (Quarterly YoY Growth) data by YCharts

Dupixent's total sales (as reported by Sanofi) were $6.5 billion, up 35% from the year-ago period. Regeneron is achieving impressive financial results despite continued pressure on its Eylea franchise. This medicine for several eye-related disorders has been facing stiff competition, biosimilar and otherwise.

But a newer, higher-dose (HD) formulation of Eylea with a more convenient dosing schedule is helping to smooth out the losses. In the third quarter, U.S. sales of Eylea HD increased 62% year over year to $699 million, although total Eylea and Eylea HD combined U.S. sales declined by 1% year over year to $1.1 billion.

Still, overall, Regeneron is performing very well, and the company could maintain solid momentum as the impact of the older version of Eylea on its financial results fades. Meanwhile, Regeneron boasts an attractive pipeline across oncology, weight loss, fatty liver disease, and other areas. The company should have a much stronger approved lineup within the next few years.

And while Dupixent could lose patent exclusivity in the early 2030s, Regeneron and Sanofi are planning to launch another formulation of the blockbuster drug that could grant it a more convenient dosing schedule and help it retain a decent market share.

That, combined with newer products it should launch by then, could allow Regeneron to continue posting solid financial results and deliver superior returns.

Europe Spent €16.5 Million Proving Again That Hydrogen Burns in a Turbine

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Hydrogen burns. Turbines spin. Their exhaust can make steam. Kawasaki demonstrated hydrogen-fired electricity and heat production at Kobe Port Island in 2018, before Europe funded the first HYFLEXPOWER programme. Six years later, Siemens Energy is still issuing announcements about hydrogen combustion at a single turbine in a French paper mill. European and British taxpayers have supplied roughly €16.5 million for the two research programmes, and the same installation has generated at least 78 identified press appearances. Neither the grants nor the publicity have produced a commercial reason to make hydrogen from electricity and burn it for industrial electricity and steam.

The latest Siemens Energy announcement, dated November 16, 2026, describes an SGT-400 turbine at Smurfit Westrock’s Saillat-sur-Vienne paper mill operating on pure hydrogen through startup, load changes and full-load cogeneration. The equipment can operate under those conditions. Hydrogen combustion and heat recovery were already established. Extending the operating range of one manufacturer’s turbine has not changed hydrogen’s production cost, the losses involved in making it or the available alternatives. No second commercial installation has emerged from the programme.

Siemens’ own March 2024 technical article supplied the most important numbers. The electrolyzer installed at the paper mill produces 16.4 kg of hydrogen per hour. The turbine consumes about 810 kg per hour when producing 10 MW of electricity. Onsite production covers 2.02% of hourly fuel demand. Nearly 50 times that electrolyzer capacity would be needed to supply the turbine continuously at the stated operating point. Later testing used hydrogen brought to the site.

The 2023 campaign accumulated about 90 hours of hydrogen and natural-gas blending tests, including just over four hours on pure hydrogen. Siemens identified limited hydrogen availability as the constraint. The 2026 announcement supplies no cumulative full-load hydrogen operating hours, delivered fuel price or routine cost of electricity and steam. These are the figures an industrial customer needs before buying equipment. Announcing another combustion test avoids the question of who will supply hundreds of kilograms of affordable hydrogen every hour for years.

My complete TFIE Strategy Briefing investigation examines the funding records, Siemens’ published operating figures, the energy balance and an audited chronology of 78 press appearances surrounding the same turbine.

At an illustrative 50 kWh of electricity per kilogram of hydrogen, manufacturing the turbine’s 810 kg of hourly fuel requires approximately 40.5 MW of electricity. The turbine returns about 10 MW of electricity plus usable exhaust heat. Compression, storage and delivery add equipment and expense. The mill needs electricity and process steam; converting electricity into gas and burning it to recover those services introduces losses and costs that direct electrification avoids.

France is an especially poor location for this detour. RTE’s 2025 electricity review reports that 95.2% of French generation was low-carbon, with average generation emissions of 19.6 gCO₂e/kWh. Direct grid power can supply the electrical load. Industrial heat pumps, mechanical vapour recompression, electric boilers and thermal storage can supply process heat and steam in combinations designed around the mill’s operating requirements. The programme should have compared these complete systems before committing public money to more hydrogen turbine retrofits.

A paper mill in Finland is already doing the industrial work. Delfort’s Tervakoski plant has operated a Turboden heat pump and mechanical vapour-recompression installation since January 2026. It supplies about 12 MW of process steam at temperatures up to 170°C, with a reported coefficient of performance around two. It delivers useful steam without a hydrogen-production plant, hydrogen deliveries or a hydrogen-fired turbine. The exact plant integration will vary, but the alternative is operating commercially while HyCoFlex continues issuing test announcements.

The funding was directed at the wrong problem. HYFLEXPOWER received €10.48 million in EU support. HyCoFlex received another €4.44 million. A Siemens presentation identifies approximately €1.56 million more in UK support, bringing the public contribution across the programmes to about €16.48 million. Both programmes were designed around making existing gas-turbine cogeneration equipment compatible with hydrogen. The grants paid for additional turbine operating conditions instead of establishing that hydrogen was a sensible way to provide the plant’s energy.

The programme’s economics were weak before the second award. In October 2023, Siemens presented a scenario requiring electricity below €35/MWh to bring hydrogen fuel costs into parity with natural gas under its 2022 cost assumptions. The HYFLEXPOWER final workshop later examined favourable operating cases with hydrogen substitution and electrolyzer utilization both above 90%. European reporting also identified renewable electricity below €40/MWh as a condition for favourable deployment economics. France’s 2025 wholesale electricity averaged €61.1/MWh. Its negative-price hours were occasional. They could not supply a plant operating more than 90% of the time. These conditions were set out before the research programme ended, yet funding continued for more combustion trials.

Siemens also has a clear commercial interest in preserving turbine markets. Its 2025 presentation explicitly described protecting existing assets and future gas-turbine investments in a decarbonizing world as a benefit of hydrogen compatibility. A turbine supplier is entitled to pursue its products with its own money. The purpose of public industrial-decarbonization funding is to reduce emissions and costs, not to protect combustion-equipment sales from direct-electric competition.

The TFIE media audit counted 19 press appearances in 2020, 13 in the 2022 installation and blending phase, 35 around the 2023 tests and award, four in 2024 and seven through November 16, 2026. Those 78 appearances trace back to a handful of announcements about one installation. They are not 78 independent engineering advances or 78 deployments. The headline count swelled while the operating fleet did not.

Public funders should require equipment-neutral comparisons of delivered industrial energy services before underwriting another hydrogen retrofit. The evidence needed is familiar: a year’s reliable electricity and steam, actual operating cost, lifecycle emissions, fuel contracts, and performance against direct-electric alternatives. HyCoFlex has supplied more hydrogen-combustion publicity without establishing those outcomes. The problem was not that Europe failed to make a turbine burn hydrogen. It was that public money kept flowing after the relevant energy and economic questions already had answers.

The full TFIE Strategy Briefing investigation contains the audited funding and media evidence, the 2% onsite hydrogen-supply calculation and the direct-electric industrial steam comparison. TFIE Strategy provides evidence-based assessments of industrial energy investments and public programmes.

Going Up: Sharp Uptick in Rates After Four Weeks of Declines

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Estate tax,Model house with Percentage symbol icon on green background,Business investment and Property tax concept

Mortgage rates moved higher this week, but experts say even against the backdrop of potential Fed rate increases and record-low home sales, there are some advantages in the housing market for buyers who remain active.

The latest Freddie Mac Primary Mortgage Market Survey® released Thursday shows the average 30-year fixed-rate mortgage rate at 7.28%, up from 7.11% last week. The 30-year FRM is still up year-over-year from 6.26% last year. The 15-year FRM averaged 6.66%, up from last week’s 6.45%. A year ago at this time, the 15-year FRM averaged 5.54%.

At press time, the average rate was up to 7.46% Thursday afternoon, according to Mortgage News Daily.

Bright MLS Chief Economist Lisa Sturtevant broke down the numbers for buyers, explaining at current interest rates, and assuming a 10% down payment and average property tax and home insurance costs, the monthly payment on the median-priced home in the U.S. is $3,100. A homebuyer needs an income of over $130,000 to qualify to purchase the median priced home. The median household income in the U.S. is currently about $90,000.  Renters have lower incomes, with a median of about $60,000, she said.

“Last year at this time, when mortgage rates were 100 basis points lower, the median mortgage payment was $250 lower. The difference is enough to price some prospective homebuyers out of the market,” Sturtevant added. “There are other would-be homebuyers who could take on this higher rate but instead are looking ahead to 2027 hoping for more favorable conditions.”

She also said the desire for homeownership remains strong, particularly among young adults, according to recent surveys. “We should expect buyers to return to the market in the coming years; however, higher rates are forcing many young adults to delay, which means they are also delaying the wealth accumulation that comes through homeownership.”

Realtor.com Senior Economist Anthony Smith said hot inflation data and the persistence of the conflict in Iran is placing upward pressure on bond yields and borrowing costs.

“The October CPI report came in above expectations on both the headline and core measures, cementing market expectations for a December rate hike and giving the 10-year little room to fall.

“The debate has shifted from whether the Fed hikes in December to how aggressively it moves,” he added, noting that backdrop is tough for mortgage rates heading into what is already a seasonally slow stretch for the housing market.

“Pending home sales fell to a record low in October, and existing home sales hit their lowest level since 2010, as the surge in rates through mid-October took its toll on buyers and sellers alike. Builder confidence has also fallen to a 14-year low, and permit activity dropped to its lowest level since the early days of the pandemic, signaling that the supply pipeline will face continued pressure in the months ahead,” Smith said.

But for buyers who remain active, Smith noted conditions offer some real advantages.

“With fewer shoppers in the market, competition has thinned, sellers have shown more flexibility on price, and active inventory continues to rise even as new listings have pulled back. The challenge is that until inflation cools enough to put mortgage rates on a durable downward path, affordability will remain the dominant constraint on the market.”

Mortgage Purchase and Refi Activity Hits New Low in Q3

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Data for the third quarter for 2026 continues to showcase the effects of high rates and elevated housing-related costs, as the latest data reveals that the quarterly slowdown was driven by declines in both home purchase and refinance lending.

ATTOM—the California-based property data and real estate analytics company—found that all mortgages and lending in housing fell in Q3 2026, in the latest U.S. Residential Property Mortgage Origination Report.

According to the report, a total of 1.29 million purchase and refinance mortgages secured by residential property (one in four units) originated in the third quarter, which is down 12% from Q2 2026 and down 15% compared to the same period last year.

“The increase in refinance activity we saw late last year and into early this year cooled in the third quarter, with refinancing declining more than purchase lending,” ATTOM CEO Rob Barber said. “Purchase loans also slipped to their lowest third-quarter level since 2012, as higher mortgage rates and elevated average purchase loan amounts continued to strain affordability for many buyers.”

Barber—alongside other economists—attribute the third quarter’s cooling housing activity to the sharp jump in mortgage rates. After the Federal Reserve’s rate hike in September, the average 30-year fixed-rate mortgage jumped to 7.40% in mid-October, and has slowly decreased but remains over 7%.

Purchase loans fell by 9% quarter-to-quarter, for a total of nearly 730,000. The report notes that this drop is the lowest third quarter since 2012. Annually, purchase loans were down 12%, although the average purchase loan increased by 3% to nearly $412,000.

Purchase lending was also down in Q3 2026, totaling $300.6 billion or down 10% from last quarter, and down 10% on a yearly basis.

While activity declined, purchase loans accounted for 56.5% of all purchase and refinance originations, which is up from the 54.5% in Q2 2026 and Q3 2025.

Looking locally, 73.6% of the analyzed metros experienced declines in purchase activity. Some cities with populations over 1 million did see quarterly increases:

But even densely-populated cities were not immune to the effect of elevated mortgage rates, with these metros experiencing the steepest quarterly drops:

Alongside purchase loans, refinance activity fell 16% quarter-to-quarter, totaling 561,290 loans. This marks the lowest quarterly total since Q2 2024, and is down 19% from the same period last year.

The share of refinance originations decreased slightly, from 45.5% in Q2 to 43.5% in Q3 2026. Although not a significant drop in shares, refinance lending declined more drastically than purchase activity by city, falling 89.4% on a quarterly basis.

The cities with the worst drops were mostly in the South and along the West Coast:

Breaking it down by government-backed activity, Federal Housing Administration (FHA) loans accounted for 15.7% of all purchase and refinance originations in the third quarter, down from the previous quarter’s 16.1% and last year’s 17.9%. VA-backed loans were also down, representing 6.9% of originations in Q3 after the previous quarter’s 8.2% and 7.2% the same period last year, or the lowest share since Q2 2024.

Again, the 2026 housing market is shaping up to have a muted end, as pending home sales and mortgage applications have been on a downward trend for the past few months.

The increase in housing construction may spell some hope for housing demand, although experts did express skepticism based on longer-term affordability challenges and economic uncertainty.

Desirae Sin is an assistant editor for RISMedia. She graduated from the University of Connecticut in 2025 with a double major in Journalism and Political Science. Prior to joining RISMedia, Desirae wrote stories geared toward policy issues for the Connecticut Mirror. She also worked as a staff writer for The Daily Campus, UConn's student-run newspaper.

GTA 6’s Best-Kept Secret Is the Most Important Part of the Game

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The prologue of GTA 6 starts with a shock

Considering how much of Grand Theft Auto 6 leaked before its launch, you’d think that Rockstar’s latest wouldn’t have anything left to hide. I mean, we’ve really seen it all. But as players immediately discovered when starting their save file, Rockstar (kind of) managed to keep one secret — and it was a huge one.

[Ed. note: This article contains spoilers for the prologue of GTA 6.]

GTA 6 kicks off with a short prologue chapter that sets a few things in motion. First, we get to see the moment Jason and Lucia met, as they both got crossed trying to jack the same car. A rom-com montage ensues, but things take a turn fast. We fast-forward to Lucia sitting in a restaurant as she prepares to pull off a robbery across the street. After nervously stalling​​​​​​​, she works up the courage to stick the place up. Unfortunately, it doesn’t go well. The cops quickly intervene and arrest her.

The twist comes as she’s being put into the back of a cop car. A panicked Lucia begs the cops to take her back to the restaurant where she was holed up before the robbery, because she left something there. The cops discover something that the camera was carefully hiding earlier in the sequence: Lucia’s infant son, Tommy, who she left unattended.

As we soon learn, Tommy is Jason and Lucia’s son — unbeknownst to Jason, until Lucia tells him upon getting out of prison. Tommy now lives with a separate guardian after getting taken from Lucia’s custody; Jason and Lucia only get to see him through occasional photos that get texted to them. It turns out that Jason and Lucia’s whole motivation for making cash in GTA 6 is to fund a family savings account in hopes that they can reunite with their son and give him a normal life.

It’s a shocking revelation that Rockstar desperately wanted to keep hidden as long as possible. No pre-release trailers for the game so much as teased that there was a kid in the picture. When I spoke to Rockstar North co-head Rob Nelson just ahead of the game’s launch, he seemed relieved that the detail had mostly gone unspoiled, despite a cavalcade of leaks. (It actually did leak about a year ago, but it faded into a forgotten rumor in a sea of them by the time GTA 6 actually launched.) For Nelson, that initial shock is an important part of an experience that’s designed to challenge what you think you know about its antiheroes at every turn.

With Tommy comes a mess of complicated character dynamics and anxieties that you’ll see throughout GTA 6’s story. While Lucia seems like an impulsive loudmouth who doesn’t have a care in the world, it becomes increasingly clear that she’s harboring serious insecurity after having Tommy taken from her. She quietly worries that she doesn’t deserve to be a parent after leaving her kid to go pull off a stick-up. While Jason wasn’t part of that, he holds the same fear that someone like him isn’t responsible enough to be a father. Those feelings bleed out whenever the two receive a text with a photo of Tommy and are reminded of the kid who is growing up somewhere without them.

Tommy also adds some welcome tension into Jason and Lucia’s love story, considering that the romance can be largely free of friction otherwise (depending on how you engage with the romance system). At one point, the two broke into a spontaneous conversation about their family. Jason noted that Lucia’s mother doesn’t seem to like him very much, which prompted Lucia to reveal that she lied to her mom and told her that Jason didn’t want to be involved in Tommy’s life. Though that isn’t exactly true given that Jason didn’t know his son existed, Lucia then recalled a conversation where Jason told her he didn’t want to have kids. That exchange gives us context that explains why Lucia didn’t tell Jason about Tommy upfront. For Nelson, those little details help build a nuanced story of two reluctant parents trying to figure out how to adapt to an unexpected responsibility.

A weekly cheat sheet to what you’ll eventually love to play. No buying, no commitment — just saving for later.

And while stories about parenting might feel a little played out in a world where the “dad game” has become a tired trope, it’s refreshing to have one that has so much compassion for everyone involved. It’s sympathetic to Lucia’s circumstances, making a point in a flashback to show her struggling to live as a single mother in a world that is hostile to her specific needs. It is careful about letting Jason face some very human insecurities without casting him as a hothead who can only deal in tough love. GTA 6 tells us that two people can survive a bad situation if they’re driven by a love that provides stability in uncertain times.

As players are bound to experience for themselves, Tommy changes so much about the psychology of GTA, even from off-screen. Maybe he won’t make you think twice about robbing a bank, but hopefully you’ll feel like all that crime isn’t for nothing every time you send a few bucks into your savings account.

Rockstar’s magnum opus searches for love and catharsis in desperate times — and finds them
